### 376 <a id="tea-dhammapada-376"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Keep company with good friends (kalyāṇa mittā), of pure livelihood and unwearied; be friendly in conduct and skilled in behaviour; then, full of gladness, you will make an end of suffering (dukkha).

> Mitte bhajassu kalyāṇe, suddhājīve atandite; Paṭisanthāravutyassa, ācārakusalo siyā; Tato pāmojjabahulo, dukkhassantaṁ karissati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: beginner · types: practice, ethics_

terms: [kalyāṇamitta](../terms/kalyanamitta.md), [dukkha](../terms/dukkha.md), [pāmojja](../terms/pamojja.md) · concepts: [Good friendship](../concepts/kalyanamittata.md)

### 377 <a id="tea-dhammapada-377"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

As the jasmine (vassikā) sheds its withered flowers, so, bhikkhus, shed passion (rāga) and hatred (dosa).

> Pañcasatabhikkhuvatthu Vassikā viya pupphāni, maddavāni pamuñcati; Evaṁ rāgañca dosañca, vippamuñcetha bhikkhavo.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

terms: [rāga](../terms/raga.md), [doṣa](../terms/dosa.md), [bhikkhu](../terms/bhikkhu.md) · obstacles: [Passion (rāga)](../obstacles/raga-pali.md), [Hate (dosa)](../obstacles/dosa.md)

### 378 <a id="tea-dhammapada-378"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Calm in body, calm in speech, calm in mind and well composed, having spat out the bait of the world (lokāmisa): a bhikkhu is called peaceful (upasanta).

> Santakāyattheravatthu Santakāyo santavāco, santavā susamāhito; Vantalokāmiso bhikkhu, “upasanto”ti vuccati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: realized · types: practice_

terms: [upasama](../terms/upasama.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md), [bhikkhu](../terms/bhikkhu.md)

### 379 <a id="tea-dhammapada-379"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Rebuke yourself by yourself, examine yourself by yourself; self-guarded and mindful, bhikkhu, you will live happily.

> Naṅgalakulattheravatthu Attanā codayattānaṁ, paṭimaṁsetha attanā; So attagutto satimā, sukhaṁ bhikkhu vihāhisi.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

### 380 <a id="tea-dhammapada-380"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Oneself is one's own protector (nātha); oneself is one's own destination (gati); therefore restrain yourself, as a merchant a fine horse.

> Attā hi attano nātho, ko hi nātho paro siyā; Attā hi attano gati, tasmā saṁyamamattānaṁ; Assaṁ bhadraṁva vāṇijo.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

### 381 <a id="tea-dhammapada-381"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The bhikkhu full of gladness (pāmojja) and confident in the Buddha's teaching would reach the peaceful state, the stilling of formations, happiness.

> Vakkalittheravatthu Pāmojjabahulo bhikkhu, pasanno buddhasāsane; Adhigacche padaṁ santaṁ, saṅkhārūpasamaṁ sukhaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

### 382 <a id="tea-dhammapada-382"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The young bhikkhu who applies himself to the Buddha's teaching lights up this world like the moon freed from a cloud.

> Sumanasāmaṇeravatthu Yo have daharo bhikkhu, yuñjati buddhasāsane; Somaṁ lokaṁ pabhāseti, abbhā muttova candimā. Bhikkhuvaggo pañcavīsatimo.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

terms: [bhikkhu](../terms/bhikkhu.md), [sāsana](../terms/sasana.md)

### 383 <a id="tea-dhammapada-383"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Cut the stream (sota) with effort, drive out sensual pleasures (kāma), brāhmaṇa; knowing the destruction of formations (saṅkhāra), you are a knower of the unmade (akataññū), brāhmaṇa.

> Chinda sotaṁ parakkamma, kāme panuda brāhmaṇa; Saṅkhārānaṁ khayaṁ ñatvā, akataññūsi brāhmaṇa.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, karma-liberation_

terms: [sota](../terms/sota-stream.md), [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [saṅkhāra](../terms/sankhara.md), [akataññū](../terms/akatannu.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 384 <a id="tea-dhammapada-384"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low

When a brāhmaṇa has gone to the far shore (pāragū) in two dhammas, then all his bonds (saṃyoga) come to an end, for he knows.

> Sambahulabhikkhuvatthu Yadā dvayesu dhammesu, pāragū hoti brāhmaṇo; Athassa sabbe saṁyogā, atthaṁ gacchanti jānato.

### 385 <a id="tea-dhammapada-385"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low

For whom there is neither the far shore, nor the near shore, nor both (pārāpāra), free of anguish (vītaddara), unfettered: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Māravatthu Yassa pāraṁ apāraṁ vā, pārāpāraṁ na vijjati; Vītaddaraṁ visaṁyuttaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: unmarked · standpoint: apophatic · path: general · stage: realized · types: ultimate_

terms: [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 386 <a id="tea-dhammapada-386"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The meditator (jhāyī), dust-free, seated, with what was to be done done, taintless (anāsava), who has reached the supreme goal (uttamattha): him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Aññatarabrāhmaṇavatthu Jhāyiṁ virajamāsīnaṁ, katakiccamanāsavaṁ; Uttamatthamanuppattaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: practice_

terms: [jhāna](../terms/jhana.md), [āsava](../terms/asava.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md) · practices: [Meditation (jhāna)](../practices/jhana.md)

### 387 <a id="tea-dhammapada-387"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

By day the sun shines, by night the moon; the warrior shines in armour, the brāhmaṇa shines meditating (jhāyī); but the Buddha shines by day and night with radiance (teja).

> Ānandattheravatthu Divā tapati ādicco, rattimābhāti candimā; Sannaddho khattiyo tapati, jhāyī tapati brāhmaṇo; Atha sabbamahorattiṁ, buddho tapati tejasā.

_level: conventional · standpoint: devotional · path: general · stage: realized · types: ultimate_

terms: [jhāna](../terms/jhana.md), [buddha](../terms/buddha.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 388 <a id="tea-dhammapada-388"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who has put away evil (pāpa) is called a brāhmaṇa; one who lives evenly (samacariyā) is called a samaṇa; one who sends away his own stain (mala) is therefore called a renunciant (pabbajita).

> Aññatarabrāhmaṇapabbajitavatthu Bāhitapāpoti brāhmaṇo, Samacariyā samaṇoti vuccati; Pabbājayamattano malaṁ, Tasmā “pabbajito”ti vuccati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [śramaṇa](../terms/sramana.md), [pabbajita](../terms/pabbajita.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md), [mala](../terms/mala-pali.md), [pabbajjā](../terms/pabbajja.md) · concepts: [Śramaṇa and brāhmaṇa](../concepts/sramana-brahmana-contrast.md)

### 389 <a id="tea-dhammapada-389"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low

One should not strike a brāhmaṇa, nor should a brāhmaṇa retaliate against him; shame on the one who strikes a brāhmaṇa, and more shame on the one who lets go at him (yassa muñcati) in return.

> Sāriputtattheravatthu Na brāhmaṇassa pahareyya, nāssa muñcetha brāhmaṇo; Dhī brāhmaṇassa hantāraṁ, tato dhī yassa muñcati.

terms: [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 390 <a id="tea-dhammapada-390"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

It is no small benefit to a brāhmaṇa when the mind is held back from the dear (piya); wherever the wish to harm (hiṁsamana) turns back, right there suffering (dukkha) subsides.

> Na brāhmaṇassetadakiñci seyyo, Yadā nisedho manaso piyehi; Yato yato hiṁsamano nivattati, Tato tato sammatimeva dukkhaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: realized · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md), [piya](../terms/piya.md), [dukkha](../terms/dukkha.md)

### 391 <a id="tea-dhammapada-391"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One in whom there is no misconduct (dukkaṭa) by body, speech or mind, restrained in these three: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Mahāpajāpatigotamīvatthu Yassa kāyena vācāya, manasā natthi dukkaṭaṁ; Saṁvutaṁ tīhi ṭhānehi, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [dukkaṭa](../terms/dukkata.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md), [saṃvara](../terms/samvara-pali.md)

### 392 <a id="tea-dhammapada-392"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

From whomever one learns the Dhamma taught by the perfectly self-awakened one, one should respectfully bow to him, as a brāhmaṇa does to the sacrificial fire.

> Sāriputtattheravatthu Yamhā dhammaṁ vijāneyya, sammāsambuddhadesitaṁ; Sakkaccaṁ taṁ namasseyya, aggihuttaṁva brāhmaṇo.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: realized · types: teacher-transmission, ethics_

terms: [dhamma](../terms/dhamma.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md), [sammāsambuddha](../terms/sammasambuddha.md)

### 393 <a id="tea-dhammapada-393"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Not by matted hair, nor by clan (gotta), nor by birth is one a brāhmaṇa; in whom there are truth (sacca) and Dhamma, he is pure, and he is a brāhmaṇa.

> Jaṭilabrāhmaṇavatthu Na jaṭāhi na gottena, na jaccā hoti brāhmaṇo; Yamhi saccañca dhammo ca, so sucī so ca brāhmaṇo.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md), [sacca](../terms/sacca.md), [dhamma](../terms/dhamma.md)

### 394 <a id="tea-dhammapada-394"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

What is matted hair to you, fool? What is a deerskin garment? Within you is a thicket; you polish the outside.

> Kuhakabrāhmaṇavatthu Kiṁ te jaṭāhi dummedha, kiṁ te ajinasāṭiyā; Abbhantaraṁ te gahanaṁ, bāhiraṁ parimajjasi.

_level: conventional · standpoint: polemical · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

### 395 <a id="tea-dhammapada-395"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The person who wears rag-robes (paṁsukūla), lean, with veins showing, meditating alone in the forest: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Kisāgotamīvatthu Paṁsukūladharaṁ jantuṁ, kisaṁ dhamanisanthataṁ; Ekaṁ vanasmiṁ jhāyantaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: practice_

terms: [paṃsukūla](../terms/pamsukula.md), [jhāna](../terms/jhana.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 396 <a id="tea-dhammapada-396"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

I do not call one a brāhmaṇa because of birth from the womb, from a mother's origin; he is just a 'bho-sayer' (bhovādī) if he has possessions (sakiñcana); the one who has nothing (akiñcana) and no grasping (anādāna): him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Ekabrāhmaṇavatthu Na cāhaṁ brāhmaṇaṁ brūmi, yonijaṁ mattisambhavaṁ; Bhovādi nāma so hoti, sace hoti sakiñcano; Akiñcanaṁ anādānaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics, dispute_

terms: [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md), [akiñcana](../terms/akincana.md), [anādāna](../terms/anadana.md) · disputes: [Is purity and worth determined by birth into a class (varṇa), or by conduct?](../debates/caste-and-purity.md)

### 397 <a id="tea-dhammapada-397"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who, having cut every fetter (saṃyojana), does not tremble (paritassati), who has gone beyond ties (saṅgātiga), unyoked: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Uggasenaseṭṭhiputtavatthu Sabbasaṁyojanaṁ chetvā, yo ve na paritassati; Saṅgātigaṁ visaṁyuttaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [saṁyojana](../terms/samyojana.md), [saṅga](../terms/sanga-pali.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 398 <a id="tea-dhammapada-398"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

One who has cut the strap and thong, the halter and bridle too, with the cross-bar lifted (ukkhittapaligha), awakened (buddha): him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Dvebrāhmaṇavatthu Chetvā naddhiṁ varattañca, sandānaṁ sahanukkamaṁ; Ukkhittapalighaṁ buddhaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [buddha](../terms/buddha.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 399 <a id="tea-dhammapada-399"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who, without ill will, endures abuse, beating and imprisonment, whose strength is patient endurance (khantībala), whose army is strength: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Akkosakabhāradvājavatthu Akkosaṁ vadhabandhañca, aduṭṭho yo titikkhati; Khantībalaṁ balānīkaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [khanti](../terms/khanti.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md) · practices: [Patience (khanti)](../practices/khanti.md)

### 400 <a id="tea-dhammapada-400"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who is not angry (akkodhana), keeps vows, is virtuous, without arrogance (anussada), tamed, whose body is his last: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Sāriputtattheravatthu Akkodhanaṁ vatavantaṁ, sīlavantaṁ anussadaṁ; Dantaṁ antimasārīraṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [kodha](../terms/kodha.md), [sīla](../terms/sila.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md) · obstacles: [Anger (kodha)](../obstacles/kodha.md)

### 401 <a id="tea-dhammapada-401"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

As water on a lotus leaf, as a mustard seed on the point of an awl, one who does not cling (limpati) to sensual pleasures (kāma): him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Uppalavaṇṇāttherīvatthu Vāri pokkharapatteva, āraggeriva sāsapo; Yo na limpati kāmesu, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 402 <a id="tea-dhammapada-402"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who knows here and now the end of his own suffering (dukkhakkhaya), who has laid down the burden, unyoked: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Aññatarabrāhmaṇavatthu Yo dukkhassa pajānāti, idheva khayamattano; Pannabhāraṁ visaṁyuttaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [dukkha](../terms/dukkha.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 403 <a id="tea-dhammapada-403"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One of deep wisdom (gambhīrapañña), intelligent, skilled in what is path and not path (maggāmagga), who has reached the supreme goal: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Khemābhikkhunīvatthu Gambhīrapaññaṁ medhāviṁ, maggāmaggassa kovidaṁ; Uttamatthamanuppattaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [paññā](../terms/panna.md), [magga](../terms/magga.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 404 <a id="tea-dhammapada-404"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who does not mingle with householders nor with the homeless, both, who wanders without a home (anokasārī), of few wishes: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Pabbhāravāsitissattheravatthu Asaṁsaṭṭhaṁ gahaṭṭhehi, anāgārehi cūbhayaṁ; Anokasārimappicchaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 405 <a id="tea-dhammapada-405"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who, having laid down the rod (daṇḍa) towards beings, moving and still, neither kills nor causes to kill: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Aññatarabhikkhuvatthu Nidhāya daṇḍaṁ bhūtesu, tasesu thāvaresu ca; Yo na hanti na ghāteti, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [daṇḍa](../terms/danda-pali.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 406 <a id="tea-dhammapada-406"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who is not hostile among the hostile, quenched (nibbuta) among those who have taken up rods, not grasping among the grasping: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Sāmaṇerānaṁvatthu Aviruddhaṁ viruddhesu, attadaṇḍesu nibbutaṁ; Sādānesu anādānaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [nibbuta](../terms/nibbuta.md), [daṇḍa](../terms/danda-pali.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 407 <a id="tea-dhammapada-407"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One in whom passion (rāga), hatred (dosa), conceit (māna) and hypocrisy (makkha) have dropped away, like a mustard seed from an awl's point: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Mahāpanthakattheravatthu Yassa rāgo ca doso ca, māno makkho ca pātito; Sāsaporiva āraggā, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [rāga](../terms/raga.md), [doṣa](../terms/dosa.md), [māna](../terms/mana.md), [makkha](../terms/makkha.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md) · obstacles: [Passion (rāga)](../obstacles/raga-pali.md), [Hate (dosa)](../obstacles/dosa.md), [Conceit (māna)](../obstacles/mana.md)

### 408 <a id="tea-dhammapada-408"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who would utter speech that is gentle (akakkasa), informative (viññāpanī) and true, by which he offends no one: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Pilindavacchattheravatthu Akakkasaṁ viññāpaniṁ, giraṁ saccamudīraye; Yāya nābhisaje kañci, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics, sound-language_

terms: [sacca](../terms/sacca.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 409 <a id="tea-dhammapada-409"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who in the world takes nothing that is not given, long or short, small or large, beautiful or ugly: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Aññatarattheravatthu Yodha dīghaṁ va rassaṁ vā, aṇuṁ thūlaṁ subhāsubhaṁ; Loke adinnaṁ nādiyati, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [adinnādāna](../terms/adinnadana.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 410 <a id="tea-dhammapada-410"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One for whom there are no hopes (āsā), in this world or the next, without hope, unyoked: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Sāriputtattheravatthu Āsā yassa na vijjanti, asmiṁ loke paramhi ca; Nirāsāsaṁ visaṁyuttaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [āsā](../terms/asa.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 411 <a id="tea-dhammapada-411"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

One who has no attachments (ālaya), free of doubt (akathaṅkathī) through knowledge, who has plunged into the deathless (amatogadha): him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Mahāmoggallānattheravatthu Yassālayā na vijjanti, aññāya akathaṅkathī; Amatogadhamanuppattaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [ālaya](../terms/alaya.md), [amata](../terms/amata.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 412 <a id="tea-dhammapada-412"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who here has gone beyond attachment (saṅga) to both merit (puñña) and evil (pāpa), sorrowless (asoka), dust-free, pure: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Revatattheravatthu Yodha puññañca pāpañca, ubho saṅgamupaccagā; Asokaṁ virajaṁ suddhaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [puñña](../terms/punna-pali.md), [pāpa](../terms/papa-pali.md), [saṅga](../terms/sanga-pali.md), [soka](../terms/soka.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 413 <a id="tea-dhammapada-413"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Like the moon, spotless, pure, serene and unclouded, with delight in existence (nandībhava) exhausted: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Candābhattheravatthu Candaṁva vimalaṁ suddhaṁ, vippasannamanāvilaṁ; Nandībhavaparikkhīṇaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [nandībhava](../terms/nandibhava.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 414 <a id="tea-dhammapada-414"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who has passed beyond this treacherous, difficult road, saṃsāra and delusion (moha); crossed over, gone to the far shore, meditating (jhāyī), unstirred (aneja), free of doubt, quenched (nibbuta) by not grasping: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Sīvalittheravatthu Yomaṁ palipathaṁ duggaṁ, saṁsāraṁ mohamaccagā; Tiṇṇo pāraṅgato jhāyī, anejo akathaṅkathī; Anupādāya nibbuto, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [saṃsāra](../terms/samsara.md), [moha](../terms/moha.md), [jhāna](../terms/jhana.md), [nibbuta](../terms/nibbuta.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md) · concepts: [Saṃsāra](../concepts/samsara.md)

### 415 <a id="tea-dhammapada-415"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who here, having abandoned sensual desire (kāma), wanders homeless (anāgāra), for whom sense-existence (kāmabhava) is exhausted: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Sundarasamuddattheravatthu Yodha kāme pahantvāna, anāgāro paribbaje; Kāmabhavaparikkhīṇaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [bhāva](../terms/bhava.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 416 <a id="tea-dhammapada-416"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who here, having abandoned craving (taṇhā), wanders homeless, for whom craving-existence (taṇhābhava) is exhausted: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Jaṭilattheravatthu Yodha taṇhaṁ pahantvāna, anāgāro paribbaje; Taṇhābhavaparikkhīṇaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ. Jotikattheravatthu Yodha taṇhaṁ pahantvāna, anāgāro paribbaje; Taṇhābhavaparikkhīṇaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [taṇhā](../terms/tanha.md), [bhāva](../terms/bhava.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md) · obstacles: [Craving (taṇhā)](../obstacles/tanha.md)

### 417 <a id="tea-dhammapada-417"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

One who has abandoned the human bond (yoga), gone beyond the divine bond, and is unyoked from every bond: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Naṭaputtakattheravatthu Hitvā mānusakaṁ yogaṁ, dibbaṁ yogaṁ upaccagā; Sabbayogavisaṁyuttaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [yoga](../terms/yoga-pali.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 418 <a id="tea-dhammapada-418"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who has given up delight and discontent (rati, arati), cooled (sītibhūta), without substrate (nirūpadhi), a hero who has overcome all the world: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Naṭaputtakattheravatthu Hitvā ratiñca aratiñca, sītibhūtaṁ nirūpadhiṁ; Sabbalokābhibhuṁ vīraṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [rati](../terms/rati-pali.md), [arati](../terms/arati.md), [sītibhūta](../terms/sitibhuta.md), [upādhi](../terms/upadhi.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 419 <a id="tea-dhammapada-419"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who knows the passing away and rebirth of beings in every way, unattached (asatta), well-gone (sugata), awakened (buddha): him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Vaṅgīsattheravatthu Cutiṁ yo vedi sattānaṁ, upapattiñca sabbaso; Asattaṁ sugataṁ buddhaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: powers-experiences, world-fate_

terms: [cuti](../terms/cuti.md), [upapatti](../terms/upapatti.md), [sugata](../terms/sugata-pali.md), [buddha](../terms/buddha.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 420 <a id="tea-dhammapada-420"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One whose destination (gati) gods, gandhabbas and humans do not know, the taint-destroyed (khīṇāsava) arahant: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Yassa gatiṁ na jānanti, devā gandhabbamānusā; Khīṇāsavaṁ arahantaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation_

terms: [gati](../terms/gati.md), [āsava](../terms/asava.md), [arahant](../terms/arahant.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 421 <a id="tea-dhammapada-421"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who has nothing (kiñcana) before, after or in the middle, who has nothing and no grasping: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Dhammadinnāttherīvatthu Yassa pure ca pacchā ca, majjhe ca natthi kiñcanaṁ; Akiñcanaṁ anādānaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ethics_

terms: [akiñcana](../terms/akincana.md), [anādāna](../terms/anadana.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 422 <a id="tea-dhammapada-422"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The bull-like one, excellent, a hero, a great seer (mahesi), a victor (vijitāvin), unstirred (aneja), washed (nhātaka), awakened (buddha): him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Aṅgulimālattheravatthu Usabhaṁ pavaraṁ vīraṁ, mahesiṁ vijitāvinaṁ; Anejaṁ nhātakaṁ buddhaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: ultimate_

terms: [buddha](../terms/buddha.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### 423 <a id="tea-dhammapada-423"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who knows his former lives (pubbenivāsa) and sees heaven and the states of loss (apāya), and has reached the end of birth (jātikkhaya), a sage (muni) who has perfected the higher knowledges (abhiññāvosita), who has fulfilled all that is to be fulfilled: him I call a brāhmaṇa.

> Devahitabrāhmaṇavatthu Pubbenivāsaṁ yo vedi, saggāpāyañca passati; Atho jātikkhayaṁ patto, abhiññāvosito muni; Sabbavositavosānaṁ, tamahaṁ brūmi brāhmaṇaṁ. Brāhmaṇavaggo chabbīsatimo. Ettāvatā sabbapaṭhame yamakavagge cuddasa vatthūni, appamādavagge nava, cittavagge nava, pupphavagge dvādasa, bālavagge pannarasa, paṇḍitavagge ekādasa, arahantavagge dasa, sahassavagge cuddasa, pāpavagge dvādasa, daṇḍavagge ekādasa, jarāvagge nava, attavagge dasa, lokavagge ekādasa, buddhavagge nava, sukhavagge aṭṭha, piyavagge nava, kodhavagge aṭṭha, malavagge dvādasa, dhammaṭṭhavagge dasa, maggavagge dvādasa, pakiṇṇakavagge nava, nirayavagge nava, nāgavagge aṭṭha, taṇhāvagge dvādasa, bhikkhuvagge dvādasa, brāhmaṇavagge cattālīsāti pañcādhikāni tīṇi vatthusatāni. Satevīsacatussatā, catusaccavibhāvinā; Satattayañca vatthūnaṁ, pañcādhikaṁ samuṭṭhitāti. Dhammapade vaggānamuddānaṁ Yamakappamādo cittaṁ, pupphaṁ bālena paṇḍito; Arahanto sahassañca, pāpaṁ daṇḍena te dasa. Jarā attā ca loko ca, buddho sukhaṁ piyena ca; Kodho malañca dhammaṭṭho, maggavaggena vīsati. Pakiṇṇaṁ nirayo nāgo, taṇhā bhikkhu ca brāhmaṇo; Ete chabbīsati vaggā, desitādiccabandhunā. Gāthānamuddānaṁ Yamake vīsati gāthā, appamādamhi dvādasa; Ekādasa cittavagge, pupphavaggamhi soḷasa. Bāle ca soḷasa gāthā, paṇḍitamhi catuddasa; Arahante dasa gāthā, sahasse honti soḷasa. Terasa pāpavaggamhi, daṇḍamhi dasa satta ca; Ekādasa jarā vagge, attavaggamhi tā dasa. Dvādasa lokavaggamhi, buddhavaggamhi ṭhārasa; Sukhe ca piyavagge ca, gāthāyo honti dvādasa. Cuddasa kodhavaggamhi, malavaggekavīsati; Sattarasa ca dhammaṭṭhe, maggavagge sattarasa. Pakiṇṇe soḷasa gāthā, niraye nāge ca cuddasa; Chabbīsa taṇhāvaggamhi, tevīsa bhikkhuvaggikā. Ekatālīsagāthāyo, brāhmaṇe vaggamuttame; Gāthāsatāni cattāri, tevīsa ca punāpare; Dhammapade nipātamhi, desitādiccabandhunāti. Dhammapadapāḷi samattā.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: realized · types: powers-experiences, karma-liberation_

terms: [pubbenivāsa](../terms/pubbenivasa.md), [jātikkhaya](../terms/jatikkhaya.md), [muni](../terms/muni.md), [abhiññā](../terms/abhinna.md), [brāhmaṇa](../terms/brahmana.md)

### ch1 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 1, Yamakavagga, verses 1-20. The chapter is built of paired (yamaka) verses, each pair setting a dark case against a bright one. 1-2: things have mind as forerunner, and suffering or happiness follows speech and action from a corrupt or a clear mind. 3-5: harbouring or not harbouring the thought 'he abused me...' and the saying that enmity is stilled by non-enmity, an ancient dhamma. 6: quarrels settle for those who understand. 7-8: living contemplating the beautiful with unrestrained faculties against contemplating the unlovely with restrained faculties, with Māra overpowering the one and not the other. 9-10: the ochre robe worn with or without self-control and truthfulness. 11-12: mistaking the unessential for the essential and the reverse. 13-14: rain breaking through an ill-roofed house as passion through an undeveloped mind. 15-18: the evildoer and the merit-maker grieving or rejoicing in both worlds. 19-20: reciting much without practice against reciting little and living by the Dhamma.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

### ch10 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 10, Daṇḍavagga, verses 129-145. The chapter is about the rod (daṇḍa). 129-132: all tremble at the rod and fear death; making oneself the comparison one should neither kill nor cause to kill; one who harms beings who wish for happiness does not gain happiness. 133-134: harsh speech brings answers in kind; one who is still like a battered gong has reached nibbāna. 135: ageing and death drive on life like a cowherd with a stick. 136-140: the fool is tormented by his own deeds, and one who harms the rodless quickly comes to one of ten states. 141-142: neither ascetic observances purify one who has not crossed doubt, and one who has laid down the rod is a brāhmaṇa, a samaṇa, a bhikkhu even if adorned. 143-145: shame and the fine horse touched by the whip, ardour with faith, virtue and the rest, and the wise taming themselves.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: ethics_

### ch11 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 11, Jarāvagga, verses 146-156. The chapter is about ageing (jarā). 146-150: why laugh when there is constant burning; the body as a painted image, a nest of disease, scattered bones, a city of bones in which ageing and death, conceit and hypocrisy are stored. 151-152: royal chariots and bodies age but the Dhamma of the good does not; a man of little learning ages like an ox. 153-154: the house-builder sought through many births is seen and will not build again. 155-156: those who did not lead the holy life or gain wealth in youth brood like old herons and lie like spent arrows.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: body-layers, death-dying_

### ch12 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch12"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 12, Attavagga, verses 157-166. The chapter is about the self (attā). 157-160: one who holds oneself dear should guard oneself, first establish oneself in what is fitting, do as one instructs, and see that oneself is one's own protector. 161-164: evil done by oneself grinds down the fool; extreme ill conduct does to oneself what an enemy would wish; the harmful is easy and the beneficial hard; reviling the teaching of the noble brings the reviler's own destruction. 165-166: purity and impurity are one's own, and one should not neglect one's own welfare for another's.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

### ch13 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch13"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 13, Lokavagga, verses 167-178. The chapter is about the world (loka). 167-169: do not follow the base dhamma, live heedlessly or hold wrong view; practise the Dhamma and live happily here and beyond. 170-171: look at the world like a bubble or a mirage, like a royal chariot in which fools sink. 172-173: one who ceases to be heedless, or covers evil with the wholesome, lights up the world like the moon from a cloud. 174-175: the world is blind, few go to heaven, and the steadfast are led out of the world having conquered Māra. 176-177: no evil is beyond a liar who disregards the world beyond, and misers do not go to the world of the gods. 178: the fruit of stream-entry is better than sovereignty of the earth or heaven.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, world-fate_

### ch14 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 14, Buddhavagga, verses 179-196. The chapter is about the Buddha (buddha). 179-180: the Buddha of endless range, trackless, cannot be led by any track. 181-182: even gods envy the meditators, and the human birth, the hearing of the Dhamma and the arising of Buddhas are hard to gain. 183-185: the teaching of the Buddhas: not doing evil, undertaking the wholesome, purifying one's mind; patient endurance as the highest austerity; the list beginning with not reviling. 186-187: no satiety in sense pleasures even by a rain of coins, and delight in the ending of craving. 188-192: refuge in mountains and trees is not safe; refuge in the Buddha, Dhamma and Saṅgha and the seeing of the four truths frees from all suffering. 193-196: the rarity of the thoroughbred person, the happiness of the arising of Buddhas, and the immeasurable merit of honouring those who have crossed sorrow.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, ultimate_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 15, Sukhavagga, verses 197-208. The chapter is about happiness (sukha). 197-200: 'happily indeed we live' without hatred among the hateful, free of affliction among the afflicted, free of eagerness among the eager, having nothing and feeding on joy. 201: victory breeds enmity and the calmed one lies happily. 202-204: no fire like passion, no ill luck like hatred, no suffering like the aggregates; hunger and formations named as the worst; health, contentment and trust praised; nibbāna the supreme happiness. 205: the taste of seclusion and of calm. 206-208: happy is the sight of the noble and living with the steadfast, and the wise, learned, virtuous good person is to be followed as the moon follows the stars.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 16, Piyavagga, verses 209-220. The chapter is about the dear (piya). 209-211: applying oneself to the wrong thing and neglecting one's goal leads to envy; not seeing the dear and seeing the undear is suffering, so one should not make anything dear. 212-216: five parallel verses tracing grief and fear to the dear, affection, delight, sensual desire and craving, and their absence to the one freed. 217-218: whom people hold dear, and the one said to be going upstream. 219-220: the returned traveller is welcomed by kin as the merit-maker is by his merits.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 17, Kodhavagga, verses 221-234. The chapter is about anger (kodha). 221-224: abandon anger and conceit and overcome every fetter; restrain arisen anger like a swerving chariot; conquer anger by non-anger, the bad by the good, the stingy by giving, the liar by truth; speak truth, do not be angry, give when asked. 225-226: the non-harming sages go to the unshakeable place, and for the ever-awake the taints end. 227-230: no one in the world is not blamed and no one wholly blamed or wholly praised; one praised by the discerning is like a coin of gold. 231-234: guarding body, speech and mind from agitation and wrong conduct.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ethics_

### ch18 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch18"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 18, Malavagga, verses 235-255. The chapter is about stains (mala). 235-238: you are like a withered leaf at the mouth of departure with no provisions; make an island for yourself and be stainless. 239-240: blow away one's stains gradually as a smith does silver, and rust eats the iron it arose from. 241-243: neglect of recitation, of exertion, sloth, heedlessness, misconduct and stinginess are stains, and ignorance is the worst stain. 244-245: life is easy for the shameless and hard for the one with shame. 246-250: killing, lying, taking what is not given, going to another's wife and drinking liquor dig up one's own root; discontent about others' food prevents concentration. 251: no fire, grip, net or river like passion, hatred, delusion and craving. 252-253: others' faults are easy to see and one's own hard; looking at others' faults makes the taints increase. 254-255: no track in the sky, no samaṇa outside, and none of wavering in the Buddhas.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 19, Dhammaṭṭhavagga, verses 256-272. The chapter is about being established in the Dhamma (dhammaṭṭha) and the true marks of titles. 256-257: one is not dhammaṭṭha by deciding by force but by leading others by Dhamma. 258-261: wise, Dhamma-holder and elder are not so by much talk or grey hair but by security and non-enmity, seeing the Dhamma with the body, and truth, Dhamma, non-harming, restraint and self-control. 262-263: eloquence or beauty does not make one of good appearance if envious, stingy or deceitful. 264-269: samaṇa, bhikkhu and muni are not so by a shaven head, by begging or by silence but by conduct. 270: one is noble by non-harming. 271-272: virtue and vows, learning, concentration or solitude are not to be rested on before the destruction of the taints.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: ethics_

Chapter 2, Appamādavagga, verses 21-32. The chapter praises heedfulness (appamāda) and censures heedlessness. 21-23: heedfulness is the path to the deathless, the wise delight in it, and the constant meditators touch nibbāna. 24-25: how repute grows for the heedful, and the island a wise person makes that no flood overwhelms. 26-29: fools devote themselves to heedlessness, the wise guard heedfulness like wealth, and the wise one who drives out heedlessness looks on the sorrowing as from a mountain top. 30: Maghavā is named as an example. 31-32: a bhikkhu who delights in heedfulness burns the fetters, cannot decline, and is near nibbāna.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 20, Maggavagga, verses 273-289. The chapter is about the path (magga). 273-275: the eightfold path is the best of paths and the only one for purification of vision; following it ends suffering. 276: one must make the effort oneself, the Tathāgatas point the way. 277-279: seeing with wisdom that all formations are impermanent, all formations suffering and all dhammas not-self brings disenchantment and is the path to purification. 280-282: the lazy do not find the path; guard speech, mind and body; wisdom grows from application. 283-285: cut the forest and the undergrowth, cut affection, foster the path of peace. 286-289: death carries off the heedless who plan for the seasons; sons and kin are no protection; purify the path to nibbāna quickly.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice_

Chapter 21, Pakiṇṇakavagga, verses 290-305. The chapter (miscellany) has no single subject. 290-291: giving up a small pleasure for a great one; happiness sought by causing pain is not free of enmity. 292-293: taints increase for the arrogant and heedless and end for those with mindfulness of the body. 294-295: two riddle verses on killing mother and father and other figures. 296-301: six parallel verses on Gotama's disciples who wake well, whose mindfulness by day and night is directed to the Buddha, Dhamma, Saṅgha, body, or whose minds delight in non-harming and development. 302: going forth is hard, household life painful. 303-305: the honour given to the faithful and virtuous, the good shining from afar, and delight in solitary faring.

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 22, Nirayavagga, verses 306-319. The chapter is about hell (niraya) and its causes. 306-308: false speakers go to hell, as do many with the ochre robe and evil states; an immoral person eating the country's alms-food is compared with swallowing a hot iron ball. 309-310: four things befall the man who consorts with another's wife. 311-315: the ascetic life wrongly handled drags one to hell as kusa grass wrongly grasped cuts the hand; lax action is not of great fruit; a bad deed better not done; guard oneself like a border city and do not let the moment pass. 316-319: through wrong view beings go to a bad destination, and through right view to a good one.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: karma-liberation, ethics_

### ch23 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch23"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 23, Nāgavagga, verses 320-333. The chapter is about the elephant (nāga). 320-323: endure abuse like an elephant in battle; the tamed is best of people and the self-tamed better than any tamed animal. 324-327: the rutting elephant and the sluggish hog as images, the mind that wandered as it wished is to be restrained thoroughly, and one should delight in heedfulness and guard one's mind. 328-330: a wise companion is to be followed, or else one fares alone like the elephant in the forest, with no companionship with a fool. 331-333: pleasant things: friends in need, contentment, merit, service of mother and father, virtue to old age, faith, wisdom, and not doing evil.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

### ch24 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch24"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 24, Taṇhāvagga, verses 334-359. The chapter is about craving (taṇhā). 334-336: craving grows like a creeper in the heedless, sorrows grow in one overcome by it and fall away from one who overcomes it. 337-338: dig up the root of craving; as a cut tree grows again, suffering arises again while the latent tendency is not uprooted. 339-341: the thirty-six streams and the creeper to be cut at the root with wisdom. 342-343: people driven by craving scurry like a trapped hare, so dispel craving. 344-347: the person freed from the forest who runs to it again, and the strong bond of longing for sons, wives and pleasure; those inflamed with passion fall into the stream like a spider into its web. 348-350: let go of before, after and middle; craving grows for one tormented by thoughts who dwells on the beautiful, and the one who delights in calming thoughts and cultivates the unlovely cuts Māra's bond. 351-354: the one free of craving with his last body and the utterance of the one freed in the destruction of craving. 355: riches destroy the dull-witted through craving for riches. 356-359: fields have weeds as their fault and the people passion, hatred, delusion, desire, so gifts to those free of them are of great fruit.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 25, Bhikkhuvagga, verses 360-382. The chapter is about the bhikkhu. 360-362: restraint of the senses, body, speech and mind, and the one restrained in hand, foot and speech, delighting inwardly. 363-367: the bhikkhu with restrained speech, who has the Dhamma as his garden, does not despise his gains or envy others, and has no sense of 'mine'. 368-370: mettā-abiding and confidence lead to the peaceful state; bail out the boat, cutting passion and hatred; the one who has gone beyond the five attachments has crossed the flood. 371-374: meditate and be not heedless, wisdom and jhāna depend on each other, and the empty dwelling, seeing the Dhamma and the rise and fall of the aggregates. 375-378: the beginning for the wise bhikkhu, good friends, shedding passion and hatred like jasmine flowers, and being peaceful. 379-382: rebuke and examine oneself, oneself is one's protector, and the young bhikkhu who applies himself lights up the world.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 26, Brāhmaṇavagga, verses 383-423. The chapter is about the brāhmaṇa; from 385 almost every verse ends 'him I call a brāhmaṇa' and names one mark of the one so called. 383-384: cut the stream, drive out sensual pleasures, and know the destruction of formations. 385-387: the one with no far shore, near shore or both, the meditator, and the Buddha shining day and night. 388-392: the titles brāhmaṇa, samaṇa, pabbajita by conduct; not striking a brāhmaṇa; respect for the teacher of the Dhamma. 393-396: not by matted hair, clan or birth, nor by matted hair and a deerskin garment; the lean rag-robed one meditating alone in the forest; the one who has nothing and no grasping. 397-416: the one freed from fetters, enduring abuse, unclinging to sensual pleasures, having laid down the rod, without hope, who has passed beyond saṃsāra and abandoned craving. 417-423: the one who has left human and divine bonds, knows the passing away and rebirth of beings, whose destination is unknown to gods and men, and who knows former lives and the end of birth.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: ultimate, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 3, Cittavagga, verses 33-43. The chapter is about the mind (citta). 33-37: the mind is quivering, unsteady and hard to guard, like a fish thrown on dry land; taming and guarding it brings happiness; those who restrain the far-going, lone, bodiless mind lying in the cave are freed from Māra's bonds. 38-39: wisdom does not become full for one with an unsteady mind, and there is no fear for one whose mind is not soaked. 40-41: the body is like a jar and the mind is made to stand like a fortified city, and the body will lie discarded like a log. 42-43: a wrongly directed mind does one worse than an enemy could, and a rightly directed mind does more good than mother, father or kin.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 4, Pupphavagga, verses 44-59. The chapter (flowers) begins with a question and its answer and then runs through flower images. 44-45: who will search out the earth and the world of Yama and gather the well-taught Dhamma-word as a skilled one gathers flowers: the trainee (sekha). 46-48: the body like foam and a mirage, and death carrying off one who gathers flowers with an attached mind. 49-50: the sage goes through a village like a bee, and looks at his own deeds rather than others' faults. 51-53: fine speech is fruitless or fruitful like a scented or unscented flower, and much wholesome should be done. 54-56: the scent of the good and of virtue goes against the wind and is unsurpassed. 57: Māra does not find the path of the virtuous and heedful. 58-59: the disciple of the perfectly awakened one outshines the blind ordinary like a lotus on a rubbish heap.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice_

### ch5 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 5, Bālavagga, verses 60-75. The chapter is about the fool (bāla). 60-63: long is saṃsāra for fools, there is no companionship with a fool, sons and wealth trouble the fool, and a fool who thinks himself wise is the one called a fool. 64-65: a fool attending on a wise person all his life is like a spoon with soup, a discerning one like the tongue. 66-69: fools do evil with a bitter fruit; a deed is well done when not regretted; the fool thinks evil sweet until it ripens. 70-72: a fool's fasting is not worth a sixteenth of the understanding of the Dhamma; evil follows like fire under ash; a fool's knowledge or notoriety (ñatta) harms him. 73-75: the fool wants unreal standing, precedence and honour, and the bhikkhu should know that the path to gain differs from the path to nibbāna.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 6, Paṇḍitavagga, verses 76-89. The chapter is about the wise (paṇḍita). 76-79: whom to attend on (one who points out faults, like a treasure-revealer), to advise and restrain others though the bad dislike it, to avoid bad friends, and to live happily with joy in the Dhamma. 80-84: the wise tame themselves as irrigators, fletchers and carpenters do their materials, are unmoved by blame and praise like a rock, are clear like a deep lake, are not moved by pleasure or pain, and wish for nothing by unrighteous means. 85-86: few reach the far shore, and those who follow the well-taught Dhamma cross the realm of death. 87-89: leaving home for homelessness, giving up the dark and developing the bright, and those with minds well developed in the factors of awakening who are extinguished in the world.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

Chapter 7, Arahantavagga, verses 90-99. The chapter describes the arahant. 90: no fever for one who has completed the journey. 91-93: the mindful do not delight in dwellings, are without hoarding, and their range is the empty and signless liberation, their track hard to trace like birds in the sky. 94-96: faculties tamed like horses, unresentful like the earth, calm in mind, speech and deed. 97: a riddle verse calling the faithless, ungrateful, house-breaker the supreme person. 98-99: delightful is the ground and delightful the forests where arahants dwell.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation, ultimate_

### ch8 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 8, Sahassavagga, verses 100-115. The chapter is organised by the number thousand. 100-102: one meaningful word, verse or Dhamma-word that calms is better than a thousand of meaningless words. 103-105: conquering oneself is better than conquering a thousand times a thousand men, and no god, gandhabba or Māra can undo it. 106-108: honouring one developed person for a moment is better than a hundred years of offering, or a year's sacrifice. 109: four things increase for one who honours elders. 110-115: one day of life of a virtuous, wise, energetic meditator, or one who sees rise and fall, the deathless state or the supreme Dhamma, is better than a hundred years without.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

### ch9 <a id="tea-dhammapada-ch9"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Chapter 9, Pāpavagga, verses 116-128. The chapter is about evil (pāpa) and its result. 116-118: hasten to the good and hold the mind back from evil; do not repeat evil, do repeat merit. 119-120: the evil person sees good fortune until evil ripens, and the good person sees evil until good ripens. 121-122: neither evil nor merit is to be thought slight, since a jar is filled by falling drops. 123-125: avoid evil like a dangerous road or poison; poison does not affect an unwounded hand; wrongdoing against the blameless returns like dust thrown against the wind. 126: destinations by kind of person. 127-128: there is no place in the sky, sea or mountains where one can be free of an evil deed or of death.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: ethics, karma-liberation_

### thesis <a id="tea-dhammapada-thesis"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The Dhammapada gathers 423 verses in 26 vaggas on a single course: mind (mano) is the forerunner and chief of things (dhammā), and suffering or happiness follows what one says and does with a corrupt or a clear mind (1-2); the mind (citta) is unsteady and needs guarding and taming (33-43), and through heedfulness (21-32), restraint of body, speech and mind (231-234), the abandonment of craving (334-359), and seeing with wisdom that all formations are impermanent and suffering and all dhammas not-self (277-279), one comes to nibbāna, the supreme happiness (203-204) and to the freedom described in the arahant and the 'brāhmaṇa' (90-99, 383-423). By the six marks of purport: (1) opening and closing: the text opens with mind as forerunner (1-2) and closes with the description of the one freed, called a brāhmaṇa by conduct rather than birth (383-423); (2) repetition: the paired and serial verses (1-20, 100-102, 110-115, 212-216, 296-301, 356-359, and the refrain 'him I call a brāhmaṇa' in most verses of 385-423), and the repeated 'oneself' verses (157-166, 379-380); (3) novelty: titles such as brāhmaṇa, samaṇa, bhikkhu, muni, thera and ariya are redefined by conduct (142, 261, 264-270, 388, 393-396); (4) stated results: happiness follows a clear mind (2), nibbāna is near (32, 372), the taints end (226, 293), the freed do not grieve (225, 336, 412); (5) praise (arthavāda): heedfulness praised (21-32), one word of Dhamma over a thousand (100-102), the fruit of stream-entry over sovereignty of the earth (178), the gift of Dhamma over all gifts (354); (6) reasoned demonstration by images: the well and badly roofed house (13-14), the fletcher and the arrow (33, 80), drops filling a jar (121-122), the cut tree that grows again (338), the wheel following the ox (1), the shadow that never leaves (2). The verses are addressed variously and often unnamed; the frame stories belong to the commentary and are not used here.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, practice, karma-liberation_
